Introduites p
ar CMake 3.30

CMP017

0

Ajoutée dans la version 3.

30.

Lorsque FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED est défini à true, FetchContent_MakeAvailable() <#command:fetchcontent_makeavailable> et FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> imposent la contrainte que leur répertoire source doit déjà être peuplé. Cette exigence a toujours été documentée, mais elle n'a pas été vérifiée ou imposée avec CMake 3.29 ou les versions antérieures. Cela a parfois conduit à des erreurs difficiles à tracer lorsqu'un projet s'attendait à ce qu'une dépendance ait été peuplée, mais que son peuplement ait été silencieusement

ignoré.

CMake 3.30 et les versions supérieures préfèrent vérifier et imposer la contrainte. Cette politique fournit une compatibilité pour les situations où l'utilisateur ne peut pas facilement empêcher FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED d'être inappropriément défini à

true.

L'ancien comportement de cette politique permet à FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED d'être défini à true même si le répertoire source d'une dépendance n'a pas été peuplé. Le nouveau comportement interrompt avec une erreur fatale si FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED est défini à true et qu'un peuplement de dépendance serait ignoré, mais que le répertoire source de cette dépendance n'existe

pas.

Cette politique a été introduite dans la version 3.30 de CMake. Elle peut être définie par cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> ou cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. Si elle n'est pas définie, CMake émet un avertissement et utilise l'ancien compor

tement.

Remarque

:
L'ancien comportement d'une politique est obsolète par définition <#manual:cmake-pol
icies(7)> et peut être
supprimé dans une ve
rsion future de CMake.

CMP016

9

Ajoutée dans la version 3.

30.

Appeler FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> avec un seul argument (le nom d'une dépendance déclarée) est

obsolète.

Avant l'introduction de FetchContent_MakeAvailable() <#command:fetchcontent_makeavailable>, les projets peuplaient le contenu précédemment déclaré (avec FetchContent_Declare() <#command:fetchcontent_declare>) en utilisant le motif suivant

: 
FetchContent_GetProperties(depname)
if(NOT
depname_POPULATED) FetchContent_Popula
te(depname) add_subdirectory(${depname_
SOURCE_DIR} ${depname_BINARY_DIR}) endif()

Le motif ci-dessus ne prend pas en charge un certain nombre de fonctionnalités qui ont été ajoutées à FetchContent <#module:FetchContent> au fil du temps. Il ignore des options comme SYSTEM et EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL qui peuvent être données à FetchContent_Declare() <#command:fetchcontent_declare>, mais ne peuvent pas être rendues connues du code projet ci-dessus. Il ne prend pas non plus en charge les fournisseurs de dépendances <#dependency-providers-overview>. Les projets devraient appeler FetchContent_MakeAvailable() <#command:fetchcontent_makeavailable> au lieu d'utiliser le motif

ci-dessus.

CMake 3.30 et les versions supérieures préfèrent rejeter les appels à FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> avec le nom d'une dépendance déclarée. Cette politique fournit une compatibilité pour les projets qui n'ont pas été mis à jour pour appeler FetchContent_MakeAvailable() <#command:fetchcontent_makeavailable> à la

place.

L'ancien comportement de cette politique permet à FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> d'être appelé avec le nom d'une dépendance déclarée. Le nouveau comportement interrompt avec une erreur fatale dans de tels

cas.

Remarque

:
Appeler FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> avec les détails complets de peuplement en tant qu'arguments de commande plutôt qu'avec seulement un nom de dépendance reste entièrement pris en charge. Seule la forme appelant FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> avec un seul argument (
le nom d'une dépendance précédemment déc
larée) est obsolète avec cette politique.

Cette politique a été introduite dans la version 3.30 de CMake. Elle peut être définie par cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> ou cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. Si elle n'est pas définie, CMake émet un avertissement et utilise l'ancien compor

tement.

Remarque

:
L'ancien comportement d'une politique est obsolète par définition <#manual:cmake-pol
icies(7)> et peut être
supprimé dans une ve
rsion future de CMake.

CMP016

8

Ajoutée dans la version 3.

30.

Le module FetchContent <#module:FetchContent> implémente les étapes directement au lieu de passer par une sous-compilation

.

CMake 3.29 et les versions antérieures implémentent FetchContent comme une sous-compilation séparée. Cela nécessitait de configurer ce projet séparé et d'utiliser un outil de construction. Cette approche peut être très lente avec certains générateurs et systèmes d'exploitation. CMake 3.30 et les versions supérieures préfèrent implémenter les étapes de téléchargement, de mise à jour et de patch directement dans le cadre du projet

principal.

Le nouveau comportement a les caractéristiques suivantes

:
  • Aucune sous-compilation n'est utilisée. Toutes les opérations sont implémentées directement à partir de l'étape de configuration CMake du projet principal. Lors de l'exécution en mode script CMake, aucun outil de construction n'a besoin d'être
    • disponible.
  • Les expressions de générateur et les variables GNU Make de la forme $(SOMEVAR) ne sont pas prises en charge. Elles ne devraient pas être utilisées dans aucun argument de FetchContent_Declare() <#command:fetchcontent_declare> ou FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate>
    • .
  • Toutes les options LOG_... et USES_TERMINAL_..., l'option QUIET et la variable FETCHCONTENT_QUIET <#variable:FETCHCONTENT_QUIET> sont ignorées. La sortie de FetchContent <#module:FetchContent> fait toujours partie de la sortie de configuration du projet principal. Cela signifie également qu'elle respecte désormais le niveau de journalisation des messages (voir CMAKE_MESSAGE_LOG_LEVEL <#variable:CMAKE_MESSAGE_LOG_LEVEL> et --log-level <#cmdoption-cmake-log-level>). Le niveau de journalisation des messages par défaut devrait être comparcapable d'utiliser QUIET avec le OLD policy setting, sauf que les avertissements seront maintenant
    • affichés.
  • Le PREFIX, TMP_DIR, STAMP_DIR, LOG_DIR, et DOWNLOAD_DIR options et leurs propriétés de répertoire associées sont ignorées. Le FetchContent <#module:FetchContent> module contrôle ces emplacements en
    • interne.
  • cmake --fresh <#cmdoption-cmake-fresh> supprimera les fichiers de tampon et de script utilisés pour le suivi et le remplissage de la dépendance. Cela forcera les étapes de téléchargement, de mise à jour et de patch de la dépendance à être ré-exécutées. Le répertoire utilisé pour les téléchargements n'est pas affecté par cmake --fresh <#cmdoption-cmake-fresh>, donc tout fichier précédemment téléchargé pour la URL download method
    • peut touj
ours être
réutilisé.

Le OLD behavior a les caractéristiques suivantes

:
  • Un sous-build est toujours utilisé pour implémenter les étapes de téléchargement, de mise à jour et de patch. Un outil de build doit être disponible, même lors de l'utilisation de FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> en mode script
    • CMake.
  • Les expressions de générateur et les variables GNU Make de la forme $(SOMEVAR) peuvent être utilisées, bien que leur utilisation soit presque toujours inappropriée. Elles sont évaluées dans le sous-build, elles ne voient donc aucune information du build
    • principal.
  • Toutes les options de journalisation, de contrôle du terminal et de répertoire liées aux étapes de téléchargement, de mise à jour ou de patch sont prises en
    • charge.
  • Si l'option QUIET est utilisée, ou si la variable FETCHCONTENT_QUIET <#variable:FETCHCONTENT_QUIET> est définie à true, les avertissements ne seront pas affichés dans la
    • sortie.
  • cmake --fresh <#cmdoption-cmake-fresh> n'a aucun effet sur les fichiers de tampon ou de
    • script de la dépendance. Les étapes pré
cédemment exécutées ne seront relancées que si
les détails de la dépendance ont changé.

Il y a de bonnes chances que les utilisateurs puissent définir la variable CMAKE_POLICY_DEFAULT_CMP0168 <#variable:CMAKE_POLICY_DEFAULT_CMP<NNNN>> à NEW pour passer globalement au NEW behavior en attendant que le projet et ses dépendances soient mis à jour, utiliser le NEW policy setting par défaut. Les projets n'utilisent généralement pas les fonctionnalités que le NEW behavior ne prend plus en charge, et même ceux qui le font fonctionneront souvent encore correctement lorsque ces options sont ignorées. Avant de définir ce comportement globalement, vérifiez si certains appels FetchContent_Declare() <#command:fetchcontent_declare> ou FetchContent_Populate() <#command:fetchcontent_populate> utilisent les options ignorées d'une manière qui changerait le comportement observable, autre que de placer des fichiers temporaires ou générés en interne à des emplacements

différents.

Cette politique a été introduite dans la version 3.30 de CMake. Elle peut être définie par cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> ou cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. Si elle n'est pas définie, CMake ne émet pas d'avertissement et utilise le OLD behav

ior.

Note

:
Le OLD behavior d'une politique est déprécié par definition <#manual:cmake-pol
icies(7)> et peut être
supprimé dans une fu
ture version de CMake.

CMP016

7

Ajouté dans la version 3.

30.

Le FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module est

supprimé.

CMake 3.29 et antérieurs fournissent un FindBoost module, mais il a besoin de mises à jour constantes pour suivre les versions amont de Boost. Boost amont 1.70 et supérieur fournissent un BoostConfig.cmake package configuration file. find_package(Boost CONFIG) trouve le package amont directement, sans le find

module.

CMake 3.30 et supérieur préfèrent ne pas fournir le FindBoost module afin que les appels find_package(Boost), sans les options CONFIG ou NO_MODULE, trouvent le BoostConfig.cmake amont directement. Cette politique fournit la compatibilité pour les projets qui n'ont pas été portés pour utiliser le package amont de

Boost.

Le OLD behavior de cette politique est que find_package(Boost) charge le FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module de CMake. Le NEW behavior est que find_package(Boost) recherche le BoostConfig.cmake

amont.

Cette politique a été introduite dans la version 3.30 de CMake. Elle peut être définie par cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> ou cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. Si elle n'est pas définie, CMake émet un avertissement et utilise le OLD behav

ior.

Note

:
Le OLD behavior d'une politique est déprécié par definition <#manual:cmake-pol
icies(7)> et peut être
supprimé dans une fu
ture version de CMake.

CMP016

6

Ajouté dans la version 3.

30.

TARGET_PROPERTY <#genex:TARGET_PROPERTY> évalue les propriétés de liaison transitivement sur les dépendances privées des bibliothèques

statiques.

Dans CMake 3.29 et antérieurs, l'expression de générateur TARGET_PROPERTY <#genex:TARGET_PROPERTY> évalue les propriétés INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS>, INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES>, et INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS> comme s'il s'agissait de Transitive Compile Properties <#transitive-compile-properties> plutôt que de Transitive Link Properties <#transitive-link-properties>, même lorsque la politique CMP0099 <#policy:CMP0099> est définie à NEW. Les dépendances privées des bibliothèques statiques, qui apparaissent dans leur INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES> protégées par des expressions de générateur LINK_ONLY <#genex:LINK_ONLY>, ne sont pas suivies. Cela est incohérent avec l'évaluation des mêmes propriétés de la cible lors de la génération du

buildsystem.

CMake 3.30 et supérieur préfèrent que TARGET_PROPERTY <#genex:TARGET_PROPERTY> évalue les propriétés INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS>, INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES>, et INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS> comme Transitive Link Properties <#transitive-link-properties> de sorte que les dépendances privées des bibliothèques statiques, qui apparaissent dans leur INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES> protégées par des expressions de générateur LINK_ONLY <#genex:LINK_ONLY>, soient suivies. Cette politique fournit la compatibilité pour les projets qui n'ont pas été mis à jour pour attendre le nouveau

comportement.

Le OLD behavior pour cette politique est que TARGET_PROPERTY <#genex:TARGET_PROPERTY> évalue les propriétés INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS>, INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES>, et INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS> comme s'il s'agissait de Transitive Compile Properties <#transitive-compile-properties> en ne suivant pas les dépendances privées des bibliothèques statiques. Le NEW behavior pour cette politique est de les évaluer comme Transitive Link Properties <#transitive-link-properties> en suivant les dépendances privées des bibliothèques

statiques.

Cette politique a été introduite dans la version 3.30 de CMake. Elle peut être définie par cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> ou cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. Si elle n'est pas définie, CMake ne émet pas d'avertissement et utilise le OLD behav

ior.

Note

:
Le OLD behavior d'une politique est déprécié par definition <#manual:cmake-pol
icies(7)> et peut être
supprimé dans une fu
ture version de CMake.

CMP016

5

Ajouté dans la version 3.

30.

enable_language() <#command:enable_language> ne doit pas être appelé avant project() <#command:project>

.

Dans CMake 3.29 et antérieurs, si un projet appelait enable_language() <#command:enable_language> avant le premier appel à project() <#command:project>, la langue serait activée mais éventuellement en utilisant des détails non définis qui étaient censés l'être. Dans CMake 3.30 et supérieur, enable_language() <#command:enable_language> préfère rejeter ce cas et s'arrêter avec une erreur fatale à la place s'il détecte que project() <#command:project> n'a pas encore été appelé. Cette politique fournit la compatibilité pour les projets qui fonctionnaient lorsque enable_language() <#command:enable_language> était appelé avant project() <#command:project> et qui n'ont pas été mis à jour pour appeler ces commandes dans l'ordre

requis.

Le OLD behavior pour cette politique est d'autoriser enable_language() <#command:enable_language> à être appelé avant project() <#command:project>. Le NEW behavior pour cette politique est d'échouer avec une erreur fatale dans de tels

cas.

Cette politique a été introduite dans la version 3.30 de CMake. Elle peut être définie par cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> ou cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. Si elle n'est pas définie, CMake émet un avertissement et utilise le OLD behav

ior.

Note

:
Le OLD behavior d'une politique est déprécié par definition <#manual:cmake-pol
icies(7)> et peut être
supprimé dans une fu
ture version de CMake.

CMP016

4

Ajouté dans la version 3.

30.

add_library() <#command:add_library> rejette les SHARED libraries lorsque la plateforme ne les prend pas en

charge.

Dans CMake 3.29 et antérieurs, sur les plateformes qui ne prennent pas en charge les bibliothèques partagées (TARGET_SUPPORTS_SHARED_LIBS <#prop_gbl:TARGET_SUPPORTS_SHARED_LIBS> est false), la commande add_library() <#command:add_library> convertit automatiquement les SHARED libraries en STATIC libraries pour aider les utilisateurs à construire des projets sur de telles plateformes. Cependant, la sémantique des bibliothèques partagées et statiques est suffisamment différente pour que cette conversion automatique ne puisse pas fonctionner en général. Les projets utilisant des bibliothèques partagées doivent être portés sur de telles plateformes au cas par

cas.

Dans CMake 3.30 et supérieur, add_library() <#command:add_library> préfère rejeter la création de bibliothèques partagées sur les plateformes qui ne les prennent pas en charge, et échouer avec un message d'erreur fatale. Cette politique fournit la compatibilité pour les projets qui fonctionnaient avec la conversion automatique en bibliothèques statiques et qui n'ont pas été mis à jour avec un port

explicite.

Le OLD behavior pour cette politique est de créer implicitement

CMP0164

Added in version 3.30.

The RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND> and RUN_OUTPUT_URI <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_URI> source file properties are now checked when generating custom commands.

In CMake 3.29 and below, custom commands generated by commands like add_custom_command() <#command:add_custom_command> or generated source files are not checked for the presence of the RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND> or RUN_OUTPUT_URI <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_URI> source file properties. This policy provides a way to get a developer warning when such properties are set on a source file that is part of a custom command.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not warn about these properties. The NEW behavior for this policy is to warn about these properties. The warning will be a developer warning, which is disabled by default in release builds.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.30. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake does not warn about the behavior change, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0163

Added in version 3.30.

The GENERATED <#prop_sf:GENERATED> source file property is now visible in all directories.

In CMake 3.29 and below, the GENERATED <#prop_sf:GENERATED> source file property, like other source file properties, was scoped in every directory separately. Although policy CMP0118 <#policy:CMP0118> allowed sources marked GENERATED in one directory to be used in other directories without manually marking them as GENERATED again, the GENERATED property was still not visible to get_property() <#command:get_property> and get_source_file_property() <#command:get_source_file_property> calls.

Whether or not a source file is generated is an all-or-nothing global property of the source: a source is either generated or it is not. CMake 3.30 and above prefer to treat the GENERATED <#prop_sf:GENERATED> source file property as globally scoped. Once it is set in one directory, it is immediately visible to get_property() <#command:get_property> and get_source_file_property() <#command:get_source_file_property> calls in other directories. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated for this behavior.

The OLD behavior of this policy is for the GENERATED source file property to be visible only in the directories in which it is set. The NEW behavior of this policy is to allow the GENERATED source file property to be visible in all directories once set in any directory. Furthermore, the NEW behavior of this policy implies the NEW behavior of policy CMP0118 <#policy:CMP0118>: the GENERATED property may be set only to boolean values, and may not be turned off once turned on.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.30. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake does not warn, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0162

Added in version 3.30.

Visual Studio Generators <#visual-studio-generators> add UseDebugLibraries indicators by default.

The "Use Debug Libraries" setting in Visual Studio projects indicates what configurations are considered debug configurations. In standalone projects, this may affect MSBuild's default selection of MSVC runtime library, optimization flags, runtime checks, and similar settings. CMake typically generates all those settings explicitly based on the project's specification, so CMake 3.29 and below do not write any UseDebugLibraries indicators to .vcxproj files.

CMake 3.30 and above prefer to write UseDebugLibraries indicators because they are useful for reference by both humans and tools, and may also affect the behavior of platform-specific SDKs. The indicator for each configuration of a target is determined as follows:

  • If the target compiles sources for a known MSVC runtime library (such as that specified by MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY <#prop_tgt:MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY>), then UseDebugLibraries is true for configurations that compile for a "Debug" runtime library, and false for others.
  • Otherwise, such as in targets created by add_custom_target() <#command:add_custom_target>, UseDebugLibraries is true for the Debug configuration, and false for others.

This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect the indicators. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and used to determine the default behavior for that target's .vcxproj file.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not generate UseDebugLibraries indicators by default. The NEW behavior for this policy is to generate UseDebugLibraries indicators by default.

If the CMAKE_VS_USE_DEBUG_LIBRARIES <#variable:CMAKE_VS_USE_DEBUG_LIBRARIES> variable and/or VS_USE_DEBUG_LIBRARIES <#prop_tgt:VS_USE_DEBUG_LIBRARIES> target property is set, it explicitly controls UseDebugLibraries generation regardless of this policy.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.30. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake does not warn, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

Policies Introduced by CMake 3.29

CMP0161

Added in version 3.29.

The CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS> variable defaults to true.

Before CMake 3.29, the CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS> variable is unset by default. When using the CPack productbuild Generator <#cpack_gen:CPack productbuild Generator>, this disables the use of the domains attribute in the productbuild Distribution XML, and falls back to the auth attribute instead. These attributes control where a productbuild package is allowed to be installed. But the auth attribute has been deprecated by Apple, so projects should migrate to using domains instead.

CMake 3.29 and above prefer to use a default value of true for CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS>, which means domains will be used by default unless the project explicitly sets CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS> to false. This policy provides compatibility with projects that enabled the CPack productbuild Generator <#cpack_gen:CPack productbuild Generator>, but did not explicitly set CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS>.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to leave CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS> unset if it hasn't been set. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use a default value of true for CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS <#variable:CPACK_PRODUCTBUILD_DOMAINS>.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.29. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note that a warning will only be emitted if the CPACK_BINARY_PRODUCTBUILD <#variable:CPACK_BINARY_<GENNAME>> variable is set to true and the project is being built for an Apple platform.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0160

Added in version 3.29.

More read-only target properties now error when trying to set them.

The set_target_properties() <#command:set_target_properties> and set_property() <#command:set_property> commands are intended to error out on all read-only properties. However, CMake 3.28 and below only did this for the following properties:

  • HEADER_SETS <#prop_tgt:HEADER_SETS>
  • INTERFACE_HEADER_SETS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_HEADER_SETS>
  • IMPORTED_GLOBAL <#prop_tgt:IMPORTED_GLOBAL>
  • MANUALLY_ADDED_DEPENDENCIES <#prop_tgt:MANUALLY_ADDED_DEPENDENCIES>
  • NAME <#prop_tgt:NAME>
  • TYPE <#prop_tgt:TYPE>

This policy enforces the read-only nature of the following target properties:

  • ALIAS_GLOBAL <#prop_tgt:ALIAS_GLOBAL>
  • BINARY_DIR <#prop_tgt:BINARY_DIR>
  • CXX_MODULE_SETS <#prop_tgt:CXX_MODULE_SETS>
  • IMPORTED <#prop_tgt:IMPORTED>
  • INTERFACE_CXX_MODULE_SETS <#prop_tgt:INTERFACE_CXX_MODULE_SETS>
  • LOCATION <#prop_tgt:LOCATION>
  • LOCATION_<CONFIG> <#prop_tgt:LOCATION_<CONFIG>>
  • SOURCE_DIR <#prop_tgt:SOURCE_DIR>

The OLD behavior for this policy is to only error out for the properties MANUALLY_ADDED_DEPENDENCIES <#prop_tgt:MANUALLY_ADDED_DEPENDENCIES>, NAME <#prop_tgt:NAME>, and TYPE <#prop_tgt:TYPE>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to error out on all target properties that are documented as read-only.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.29. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0159

Added in version 3.29.

The RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND> and RUN_OUTPUT_URI <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_URI> properties are now checked when generating custom commands.

In CMake 3.28 and below, custom commands generated by commands like add_custom_command() <#command:add_custom_command> or generated source files are not checked for the presence of the RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_COMMAND> or RUN_OUTPUT_URI <#prop_sf:RUN_OUTPUT_URI> source file properties. This policy provides a way to get a developer warning when such properties are set on a source file that is part of a custom command.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not warn about these properties. The NEW behavior for this policy is to warn about these properties. The warning will be a developer warning, which is disabled by default in release builds.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.29. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0158

Added in version 3.29.

The install() <#command:install> command does not generate default installation rules for static libraries.

In CMake 3.28 and below, the install() <#command:install> command would generate default installation rules for static libraries, even if the library was never linked against by any other target. This could lead to unnecessary installation rules being generated, and potentially to issues if the library was not intended to be installed.

CMake 3.29 and above prefer to not generate default installation rules for static libraries unless they are explicitly requested or are linked against by another target. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to generate default installation rules for static libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to not generate default installation rules for static libraries unless they are explicitly requested or are linked against by another target.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.29. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

Policies Introduced by CMake 3.28

CMP0157

Added in version 3.28.

The FindPCI <#module:FindPCI> module now installs the pcilib library.

The FindPCI <#module:FindPCI> module provides macros for finding and using the PCI library. In CMake 3.27 and below, this module did not install the pcilib library, even if it was found. This could lead to issues when trying to use the library in a project that relied on it being installed.

CMake 3.28 and above prefer to install the pcilib library when it is found by the FindPCI <#module:FindPCI> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not install the pcilib library. The NEW behavior for this policy is to install the pcilib library.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.28. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0156

Added in version 3.28.

The CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM <#variable:CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM> variable's default value is now BOTH.

In CMake 3.27 and below, the default value for CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM <#variable:CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM> was NEVER. This meant that programs (executables) were never searched for in the CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH (e.g. when cross-compiling). This could lead to issues when trying to find programs that were part of the cross-compiling toolchain.

CMake 3.28 and above prefer to use a default value of BOTH for CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM <#variable:CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM>. This means that programs will be searched for in both the CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH and the system's default search paths. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to use a default value of NEVER for CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM <#variable:CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use a default value of BOTH.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.28. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0155

Added in version 3.28.

The FindPkgConfig <#module:FindPkgConfig> module now sets PKG_CONFIG_FOUND.

In CMake 3.27 and below, the FindPkgConfig <#module:FindPkgConfig> module did not set the PKG_CONFIG_FOUND <#variable:PKG_CONFIG_FOUND> variable. This could lead to issues when trying to determine if pkg-config was available in a project that relied on this variable.

CMake 3.28 and above prefer to set PKG_CONFIG_FOUND <#variable:PKG_CONFIG_FOUND> to true when pkg-config is found by the FindPkgConfig <#module:FindPkgConfig> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.28. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Policies Introduced by CMake 3.27

CMP0151

Added in version 3.27.

The CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> variable is now respected when installing.

In CMake 3.26 and below, the CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> variable was ignored when installing libraries. This meant that the RPATH would not be set at install time, which could lead to issues when trying to run the installed binaries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.27 and above prefer to respect the CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> variable when installing libraries. This means that the RPATH will be set at install time, which can help to avoid issues when trying to run the installed binaries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to ignore CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> when installing libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to respect CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> when installing libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.27. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0150

Added in version 3.27.

The FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module now prefers to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

In CMake 3.26 and below, the FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module would by default link against shared libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to link against static Boost libraries, as the module would not automatically select the correct libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.27 and above prefer to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries. This means that if Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> is set to true, the module will link against static libraries. If it is set to false, the module will link against shared libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to always link against shared libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.27. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0149

Added in version 3.27.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now uses the Python_USE_FILE <#variable:Python_USE_FILE> variable.

In CMake 3.26 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module did not use the Python_USE_FILE <#variable:Python_USE_FILE> variable. This meant that projects could not easily customize how Python 3 was found. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.27 and above prefer to use the Python_USE_FILE <#variable:Python_USE_FILE> variable when finding Python 3. This means that projects can now specify a custom file to be used for finding Python 3, allowing for more flexibility. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not use Python_USE_FILE <#variable:Python_USE_FILE>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use Python_USE_FILE <#variable:Python_USE_FILE>.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.27. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0148

Added in version 3.27.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now prefers to find Python 3 using the Python launcher.

In CMake 3.26 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module preferred to find Python 3 using the `python3` command. This could lead to issues when trying to find Python 3 on systems where `python3` is not in the PATH or points to an undesired version. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.27 and above prefer to find Python 3 using the Python launcher (e.g. `py -3` on Windows). This allows for more robust detection of Python 3 installations. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the `python3` command. The NEW behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the Python launcher.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.27. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0147

Added in version 3.27.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now sets PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` to true when the package is found.

In CMake 3.26 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module did not set the PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> variable. This meant that projects could not easily determine if a package was found using this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.27 and above prefer to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when a package is found using the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module. This allows projects to easily determine if a package was found. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when the package is found.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.27. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0146

Added in version 3.27.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now handles empty find modules.

In CMake 3.26 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module would error out if the find module it was called from was empty. This could lead to issues when trying to use this module in conjunction with other find modules. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.27 and above prefer to handle empty find modules gracefully. This means that the module will not error out if the find module is empty, allowing for more flexible use of this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to error out on empty find modules. The NEW behavior for this policy is to handle empty find modules gracefully.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.27. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0145

Added in version 3.26.

The FindQt <#module:FindQt> module now prefers to find Qt installations using the CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH.

In CMake 3.25 and below, the FindQt <#module:FindQt> module preferred to find Qt installations using the `qmake` executable. This could lead to issues when trying to find Qt installations on systems where `qmake` was not in the PATH or pointed to an undesired version. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.26 and above prefer to find Qt installations using the CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH variable. This allows for more robust detection of Qt installations, as it can be set explicitly by the user. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Qt installations using the `qmake` executable. The NEW behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Qt installations using the CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH variable.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.26. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.26. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0134

Added in version 3.26.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now prefers to find Python 3 using the Python launcher on Windows.

In CMake 3.25 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module preferred to find Python 3 using the `python3` command. This could lead to issues on Windows where `python3` might not be in the PATH or might point to an undesired version. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.26 and above prefer to find Python 3 using the Python launcher (e.g. `py -3` on Windows). This allows for more robust detection of Python 3 installations. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the `python3` command. The NEW behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the Python launcher.

CMP0133

Added in version 3.26.

The CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> variable is now respected when installing.

In CMake 3.25 and below, the CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> variable was ignored when installing libraries. This meant that the RPATH would not be set at install time, which could lead to issues when trying to run the installed binaries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.26 and above prefer to respect the CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> variable when installing libraries. This means that the RPATH will be set at install time, which can help to avoid issues when trying to run the installed binaries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to ignore CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> when installing libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to respect CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW <#variable:CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_NOW> when installing libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.26. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0132

Added in version 3.26.

The FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module now prefers to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

In CMake 3.25 and below, the FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module would by default link against shared libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to link against static Boost libraries, as the module would not automatically select the correct libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.26 and above prefer to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries. This means that if Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> is set to true, the module will link against static libraries. If it is set to false, the module will link against shared libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to always link against shared libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.26. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0131

Added in version 3.26.

The FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module now installs the libarchive library.

In CMake 3.25 and below, the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module did not install the libarchive library, even if it was found. This could lead to issues when trying to use the library in a project that relied on it being installed.

CMake 3.26 and above prefer to install the libarchive library when it is found by the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not install the libarchive library. The NEW behavior for this policy is to install the libarchive library.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.26. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

CMP0119

CMP0118

Added in version 3.25.

The GENERATED <#prop_sf:GENERATED> source file property is now visible in all directories.

In CMake 3.24 and below, the GENERATED <#prop_sf:GENERATED> source file property, like other source file properties, was scoped in every directory separately. Although policy CMP0118 <#policy:CMP0118> allowed sources marked GENERATED in one directory to be used in other directories without manually marking them as GENERATED again, the GENERATED property was still not visible to get_property() <#command:get_property> and get_source_file_property() <#command:get_source_file_property> calls.

Whether or not a source file is generated is an all-or-nothing global property of the source: a source is either generated or it is not. CMake 3.25 and above prefer to treat the GENERATED <#prop_sf:GENERATED> source file property as globally scoped. Once it is set in one directory, it is immediately visible to get_property() <#command:get_property> and get_source_file_property() <#command:get_source_file_property> calls in other directories. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated for this behavior.

The OLD behavior of this policy is for the GENERATED source file property to be visible only in the directories in which it is set. The NEW behavior of this policy is to allow the GENERATED source file property to be visible in all directories once set in any directory. Furthermore, the NEW behavior of this policy implies the NEW behavior of policy CMP0118 <#policy:CMP0118>: the GENERATED property may be set only to boolean values, and may not be turned off once turned on.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.25. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake does not warn, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

Added in version 3.25.

The FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module now installs the libarchive library.

In CMake 3.24 and below, the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module did not install the libarchive library, even if it was found. This could lead to issues when trying to use the library in a project that relied on it being installed.

CMake 3.25 and above prefer to install the libarchive library when it is found by the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not install the libarchive library. The NEW behavior for this policy is to install the libarchive library.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.25. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0111

Added in version 3.25.

The FindZLIB <#module:FindZLIB> module now installs the zlib library.

In CMake 3.24 and below, the FindZLIB <#module:FindZLIB> module did not install the zlib library, even if it was found. This could lead to issues when trying to use the library in a project that relied on it being installed.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0110

Added in version 3.25.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now sets PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` to true when the package is found.

In CMake 3.24 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module did not set the PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> variable. This meant that projects could not easily determine if a package was found using this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.25 and above prefer to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when a package is found using the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module. This allows projects to easily determine if a package was found. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when the package is found.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.25. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0109

Added in version 3.25.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now handles empty find modules.

In CMake 3.24 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module would error out if the find module it was called from was empty. This could lead to issues when trying to use this module in conjunction with other find modules. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.25 and above prefer to handle empty find modules gracefully. This means that the module will not error out if the find module is empty, allowing for more flexible use of this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to error out on empty find modules. The NEW behavior for this policy is to handle empty find modules gracefully.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.25. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

Policies Introduced by CMake 3.24

CMP0108

Added in version 3.24.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now prefers to find Python 3 using the Python launcher on Windows.

In CMake 3.23 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module preferred to find Python 3 using the `python3` command. This could lead to issues on Windows where `python3` might not be in the PATH or might point to an undesired version. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.24 and above prefer to find Python 3 using the Python launcher (e.g. `py -3` on Windows). This allows for more robust detection of Python 3 installations. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the `python3` command. The NEW behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the Python launcher.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.24. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0107

Added in version 3.24.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now detects Python 3 development headers and libraries.

In CMake 3.23 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module did not detect Python 3 development headers and libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to build Python extensions or other projects that require access to the Python C API. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.24 and above prefer to detect Python 3 development headers and libraries when the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module is used. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not detect Python 3 development headers and libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to detect Python 3 development headers and libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.24. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0106

Added in version 3.24.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now sets Python3_FOUND.

In CMake 3.23 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module did not set the Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND> variable. This could lead to issues when trying to determine if Python 3 was available in a project that relied on this variable. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.24 and above prefer to set Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND> to true when Python 3 is found by the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not set Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to set Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND> to true when Python 3 is found.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.24. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0105

Added in version 3.24.

The FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module now prefers to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

In CMake 3.23 and below, the FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module would by default link against shared libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to link against static Boost libraries, as the module would not automatically select the correct libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.24 and above prefer to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries. This means that if Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> is set to true, the module will link against static libraries. If it is set to false, the module will link against shared libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to always link against shared libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.24. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

In CMake 3.22 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module did not set the Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND> variable. This could lead to issues when trying to determine if Python 3 was available in a project that relied on this variable. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.23 and above prefer to set Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND> to true when Python 3 is found by the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not set Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to set Python3_FOUND <#variable:Python3_FOUND> to true when Python 3 is found.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.23. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0088

Added in version 3.23.

The FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module now prefers to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

In CMake 3.22 and below, the FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module would by default link against shared libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to link against static Boost libraries, as the module would not automatically select the correct libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.23 and above prefer to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries. This means that if Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> is set to true, the module will link against static libraries. If it is set to false, the module will link against shared libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to always link against shared libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.23. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0087

Added in version 3.23.

The FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module now installs the libarchive library.

In CMake 3.22 and below, the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module did not install the libarchive library, even if it was found. This could lead to issues when trying to use the library in a project that relied on it being installed.

CMake 3.23 and above prefer to install the libarchive library when it is found by the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not install the libarchive library. The NEW behavior for this policy is to install the libarchive library.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.23. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0085

Added in version 3.23.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now sets PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` to true when the package is found.

In CMake 3.22 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module did not set the PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> variable. This meant that projects could not easily determine if a package was found using this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.23 and above prefer to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when a package is found using the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module. This allows projects to easily determine if a package was found. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when the package is found.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.23. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0084

Added in version 3.23.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now handles empty find modules.

In CMake 3.22 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module would error out if the find module it was called from was empty. This could lead to issues when trying to use this module in conjunction with other find modules. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.23 and above prefer to handle empty find modules gracefully. This means that the module will not error out if the find module is empty, allowing for more flexible use of this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to error out on empty find modules. The NEW behavior for this policy is to handle empty find modules gracefully.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.23. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

Policies Introduced by CMake 3.22

CMP0078

Added in version 3.22.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now prefers to find Python 3 using the Python launcher on Windows.

In CMake 3.21 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module preferred to find Python 3 using the `python3` command. This could lead to issues on Windows where `python3` might not be in the PATH or might point to an undesired version. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.22 and above prefer to find Python 3 using the Python launcher (e.g. `py -3` on Windows). This allows for more robust detection of Python 3 installations. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the `python3` command. The NEW behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the Python launcher.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.22. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0077

Added in version 3.22.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now detects Python 3 development headers and libraries.

In CMake 3.21 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module did not detect Python 3 development headers and libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to build Python extensions or other projects that require access to the Python C API. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.22 and above prefer to detect Python 3 development headers and libraries when the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module is used. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not detect Python 3 development headers and libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to detect Python 3 development headers and libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.22. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.21. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0066

Added in version 3.21.

The FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module now installs the libarchive library.

In CMake 3.20 and below, the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module did not install the libarchive library, even if it was found. This could lead to issues when trying to use the library in a project that relied on it being installed.

CMake 3.21 and above prefer to install the libarchive library when it is found by the FindLibArchive <#module:FindLibArchive> module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior. The policy setting is recorded by each target as it is created and is used to determine the default behavior for that target's installation rules.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not install the libarchive library. The NEW behavior for this policy is to install the libarchive library.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.21. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0064

Added in version 3.21.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now sets PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` to true when the package is found.

In CMake 3.20 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module did not set the PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> variable. This meant that projects could not easily determine if a package was found using this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.21 and above prefer to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when a package is found using the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module. This allows projects to easily determine if a package was found. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>>. The NEW behavior for this policy is to set PACKAGE_FIND_`<PackageName>` <#variable:PACKAGE_FIND_<PackageName>> to true when the package is found.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.21. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0063

Added in version 3.21.

The FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module now handles empty find modules.

In CMake 3.20 and below, the FindPackageHandleStandardArgs <#module:FindPackageHandleStandardArgs> module would error out if the find module it was called from was empty. This could lead to issues when trying to use this module in conjunction with other find modules. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.21 and above prefer to handle empty find modules gracefully. This means that the module will not error out if the find module is empty, allowing for more flexible use of this module. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to error out on empty find modules. The NEW behavior for this policy is to handle empty find modules gracefully.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.21. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

Note:

The OLD behavior of a policy is deprecated by definition <#manual:cmake-policies(7)> and may be removed in a future version of CMake.

CMP0062

Added in version 3.20.

The FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module now prefers to find Python 3 using the Python launcher on Windows.

In CMake 3.19 and below, the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module preferred to find Python 3 using the `python3` command. This could lead to issues on Windows where `python3` might not be in the PATH or might point to an undesired version. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.20 and above prefer to find Python 3 using the Python launcher (e.g. `py -3` on Windows). This allows for more robust detection of Python 3 installations. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the `python3` command. The NEW behavior for this policy is to prefer finding Python 3 using the Python launcher.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.20. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

This could lead to issues when trying to build Python extensions or other projects that require access to the Python C API. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

CMake 3.20 and above prefer to detect Python 3 development headers and libraries when the FindPython3 <#module:FindPython3> module is used. This policy provides compatibility for projects that have not been updated to expect this behavior.

The OLD behavior for this policy is to not detect Python 3 development headers and libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to detect Python 3 development headers and libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.20. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.

CMP0059

Added in version 3.20.

The FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module now prefers to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

In CMake 3.19 and below, the FindBoost <#module:FindBoost> module would by default link against shared libraries. This could lead to issues when trying to link against static Boost libraries, as the module would not automatically select the correct libraries. This policy provides compatibility for projects that rely on the old behavior.

This means that if Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> is set to true, the module will link against static libraries. The OLD behavior for this policy is to always link against shared libraries. The NEW behavior for this policy is to use Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS <#variable:Boost_USE_STATIC_LIBS> to determine whether to link against static or shared libraries.

This policy was introduced in CMake version 3.20. It may be set by cmake_policy() <#command:cmake_policy> or cmake_minimum_required() <#command:cmake_minimum_required>. If it is not set, CMake warns, and uses OLD behavior.
